\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\left(\varepsilon^{1/4}\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{1/2}_{2,\infty}}+\mathsf{L}_{2}\mathsf{M}^{1/2}\frac{h}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}+\mathsf{L}_{2}\mathsf{D}^{1/2}\varepsilon^{1/2}\right).

We minimize the first two terms with respect to
\varepsilon
. That is, we put

\varepsilon=\frac{h^{4/3}}{1+\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{1/2}_{2,\infty}}^{4/3}}\leq 1\,.

This gives the bound, for an updated constant
C_{d}>0
,

\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-\varrho_{f}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{d}\,h^{1/3}\,\Big(1+\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{1/2}_{2,\infty}}^{2/3}\Big)\left(1+\mathsf{L}_{2}\mathsf{M}^{1/2}+\mathsf{L}_{2}\mathsf{D}^{1/2}h^{1/3}\right).

Secondly, we take
q=1
in Inequality (
87
) which gives, for a constant
C_{d}>0

\displaystyle\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-\varrho_{f}}\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\varrho_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}-\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}+\left\|{\varrho_{f}-\varrho_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\left(\left(\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}+\mathsf{L}_{1}\mathsf{D}^{1/2}\right)\varepsilon^{1/2}+\mathsf{L}_{1}\mathsf{M}^{1/2}\frac{h}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}\right).

We choose
\varepsilon
as

\varepsilon=\frac{h}{1+\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}}\leq 1

which gives, for a new constant
C_{d}>0

\displaystyle\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-\varrho_{f}}\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\,h^{1/2}\left(1+\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}^{1/2}+\mathsf{D}^{1/2}\right)\left(1+\mathsf{L}_{1}+\mathsf{M}^{1/2}\right)

which finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

The first inequality follows directly from (
88
) by putting
\varepsilon=h
. As a consequence, we obtain also thanks to the triangle inequality and
\tilde{f}=G_{\varepsilon}*f
with
f\in\dot{B}_{2,\infty}^{1/2}

\left\|{f-f_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\left\|{f-\tilde{f}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|{\tilde{f}-f_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{d}\,h^{1/4}\left\|{f}\right\|_{\dot{B}_{2,\infty}^{1/2}}+\left\|{f-\tilde{f}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}^{1/2}.

Let us now prove the third inequality. It follows from
[
38
, Equation (50)]
that with
\varepsilon=h

\left\|{\tilde{\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}\leq C_{d}\,\sqrt{h}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\left\|{\,\tilde{\!{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}_{g}-{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{g}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}\leq C_{d}\,\sqrt{h}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{g}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}

with
C_{d}=\frac{2d\,\omega_{2d}}{\left(2d+1\right)\omega_{2d+1}}
and
\left|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right|^{2}=\left|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right|^{2}+\left|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!\xi}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right|^{2}
. Therefore, taking
g=\tilde{f}
, it follows by the triangle inequality for the trace norm that

\left\|{{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{f}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}\leq C_{d}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{\tilde{f}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}\right)h^{1/2}+\left\|{\,\tilde{\!{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}_{f}-\tilde{\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}

which by the properties of the semiclassical convolution implies that

\left\|{{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{f}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}\leq C_{d}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{\tilde{f}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}\right)h^{1/2}+\left\|{f-\tilde{f}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})}\,.

The desired estimate follows by
\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{\tilde{f}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}=\left\|{\,\tilde{\!{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}_{\nabla f}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}\leq\left\|{\nabla f}\right\|_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})}
.
∎

Finally, we turn to the proof of Theorem
1.4
. Let us first argue that the limiting state is in both
BV(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
and
B_{2,\infty}^{1/2}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
. To see this, consider
F(x,\xi)=\mathds{1}_{\left|{\xi}\right|\leq\rho(x)^{1/d}}
for some
\rho\geq 0
with
\rho\in W^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{\frac{d-1}{d}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then, for any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}F(x,\xi)\,\nabla_{x}\varphi(x,\xi)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla\rho(x)\left(\int_{\mathds{S}^{d-1}}\varphi(x,\rho(x)^{1/d}\sigma)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\sigma\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}F(x,\xi)\nabla_{\xi}\varphi(x,\xi)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho(x)^{\frac{d-1}{d}}\left(\int_{\mathds{S}^{d-1}}\varphi(x,\rho(x)^{1/d}\sigma)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\sigma\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\ .

Thus,
\nabla F\in\mathcal{M}
and so
F
is
BV
. In addition, since
F
is a characteristic function, it follows that
F\in B_{p,\infty}^{1/p}
for all
1\leq p<\infty
. See e.g.
[
54
, Theorem 2]
.

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

Let
V
satisfy (
H1
) with parameter
\beta\geq 0
in
d=3
. In view of Proposition
4.1
and
4.2
, it suffices only to estimate the quantities
\mathsf{M}
,
\mathsf{L}_{q}
and
\mathsf{D}
.
To this end, recall
\varrho_{f}=\omega_{d}\,V_{-}^{3/2}\in W^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
is continuous and compactly supported.
Thus,
f\in B_{2,\infty}^{1/2}
and
\nabla f\in\mathcal{M}
. Without loss of generality, we assume
\beta\leq 1/8\hbar
. Otherwise, we may proceed as in the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

First, thanks to the CLR bound (
25
) and the compact support of
\varrho_{f}
, it is clear that
\mathsf{M}
is bounded uniformly in
\hbar
. Additionally, thanks to Agmon’s estimates (
35
), we have
\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\leq C
uniformly in
\hbar
. From Lemma
2.1
we also get that

\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left(1+\left\|{V_{-}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|{Ve^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\right).

Thanks to Proposition
2.4
, we can control
\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}
. Thus,
\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\in L^{\infty}
uniformly in
\hbar
and we conclude that
\mathsf{L}_{q}
is controlled for
q=1
and
q=2
. Finally, for
\mathsf{D}
we note that if
V
satisfies (
H1
), then, thanks to
\varrho_{f}\in W^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\left|{\nabla V_{\pm}}\right|\leq\left|{\nabla V}\right|
, we get

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\nabla V_{-}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|{e^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}\nabla V_{+}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\right)\left(1+\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{f}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\right)\left(1+\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\right)

for some constant
C>0
independent of
\hbar
. Finally, we recall that thanks to Theorem
1.1
, we get
\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\leq C\left|{\ln\hbar}\right|
. Thus,
\mathsf{D}^{1/2}h^{1/3}\leq C
for all
\hbar\in(0,1)
. This finishes the proof.
∎

5.
Regularity of the position density

In this section we establish further regularity properties of the position density
\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
. These estimates will be particularly helpful when we study interacting particle systems with Coulomb potentials. First, we note that it follows from the decay of
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
in the
\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|
direction that
\hbar\,\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is in
L^{\infty}
uniformly in
\hbar
, as the following lemma shows.

Lemma 5.1
.

In dimension
d\leq 3
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{d}}{\hbar}\left(1+\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{5/4}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla^{3}\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\,C_{d}}{\hbar^{3}}\left(1+\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{6}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{7/6}\right),

with
C_{d}=4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle x\right\rangle^{-4}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x=\frac{8\,\pi^{2}}{\left(4-d\right)\omega_{4-d}}
. Additionally, in dimension
d\geq 3

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{d}}{\hbar}\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla^{3}\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{d}}{\hbar^{3}}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}^{3}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}^{2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\right)

with
C_{d}=16\,\mathsf{L}_{0,d}
. If
V
has four locally bounded derivatives, the last factor of the right-hand side of both inequalities can be controlled thanks to Lemma
2.7
and Lemma
2.9
.

Proof.

We proceed by duality. Let
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be such that
\left\|{\varphi}\right\|_{L^{1}}=1
. Then

\left|{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\varphi}\right|=\left|{\frac{i}{\hbar}\,h^{d}\tr\!\left(\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\varphi-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\boldsymbol{p}\,\varphi\right)}\right|\leq\frac{2}{\hbar}\,h^{d}\left|{\tr\!\left(\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\varphi\right)}\right|.

Moreover, by the cyclicity of the trace, Hölder’s inequality and the fact that
\frac{\varphi}{\left|{\varphi}\right|}\,\mathds{1}_{\varphi\neq 0}
is a bounded multiplication operator with norm
1
, it follows that

\displaystyle h^{d}\left|{\tr\!\left(\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\varphi\right)}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|{h^{d}\tr\!\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{2}\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{-2}\,\varphi\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{-2}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{2}\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{-2}\sqrt{\left|{\varphi}\right|}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, it follows from Hölder’s inequality for Schatten norms together with the Araki–Lieb–Thirring inequality (or more precisely the Heinz inequality
[
31
]
) that for any
k\in[0,4]
,

\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{k}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\leq\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{k/4}.

Putting all these inequalities together yields

\left|{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\varphi}\right|\leq\frac{2}{\hbar}\left(\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{1/4}+\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{3/4}\right)\left(1+\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{1/2}\right)\left\|{\left\langle x\right\rangle^{-2}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

where we used the fact that
\left\|{\varphi}\right\|_{L^{1}}=1
. This gives the first inequality. The second inequality follows analogously.

For the second part, we proceed analogously with
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}
so that
\left\|{\varphi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}=1
. We get thanks to the CLR bound (
25
)

\left|{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\varphi}\right|\leq\frac{2}{\hbar}\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}\leq\frac{2\,\mathsf{L}_{0,d}}{\hbar}\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}.

For the three derivatives, we expand the commutator (here understood as tensors)

\left[{\boldsymbol{p},\left[{\boldsymbol{p},\left[{\boldsymbol{p},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right]}\right]=\boldsymbol{p}^{3}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-3\,\boldsymbol{p}^{2}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\boldsymbol{p}+3\,\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\boldsymbol{p}^{2}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\boldsymbol{p}^{3}

and proceed analogously.
∎

In particular it follows from the above lemma and Inequality (
65
) that for any
p\in[1,\infty]
,

\hbar^{1/p^{\prime}}\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{p}}\leq C_{d}^{1/p^{\prime}}\left(1+\lVert\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{5/4}\right)^{1/p^{\prime}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}^{1/p}

which is bounded uniformly in
\hbar
whenever
\lVert\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}
and
\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}
are. However, in the case of nonlinear interactions with a Coulomb potential, we can only estimate
\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}
by a term diverging like
\left|{\ln\hbar}\right|^{1/2}
. We are still able to prove however that
\hbar^{1/p^{\prime}}\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{p}}
is bounded uniformly in
\hbar
for
p\in[2,\infty]
by considering weighted Hilbert–Schmidt commutator estimates. This is the content of the next proposition.

Proposition 5.2

(
L^{2}
regularity of
\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
)
.

Let
\hbar\in(0,1)
and
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\mathds{1}_{H\leq 0}
with
H=-\hbar^{2}\Delta+V
in
d=3
. Assume that
V
verifies (
H1
) and, additionally, that there exists
C>0
and
\beta\geq 0
independent of
\hbar
such that for any
0\leq n\leq 5

(H4)

e^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}\left|{\nabla^{n}V(x)}\right|\leq C\,\hbar^{-(n-1)_{+}}\,.

Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\left\|{T_{x}\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\min\!\left(\tfrac{\left|{x}\right|}{\hbar},\sqrt{\left|{x}\right|},1\right).

In particular, for any
s<1/2
,

\sqrt{\hbar}\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}},\qquad\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{B^{1/2}_{2,\infty}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{H^{s}}

are bounded uniformly in
\hbar
.

One of the main ingredients of the proof is the following weighted variant of Proposition
3.3
for Hilbert–Schmidt estimates.

Proposition 5.3

(Weighted Hilbert–Schmidt bounds)
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\mathds{1}_{H\leq 0}
and
H=-\hbar^{2}\Delta+V
with
d\geq 1
. In addition, let
A
be a normal operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and let
m
be an operator in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with polynomial growth in
x
or
\boldsymbol{p}
. Then for any
\lambda>0
,

\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{A,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|^{2}m\right)\leq\sum_{\mathrm{i}=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\left[{{\boldsymbol{b}}_{\mathrm{i}},H}\right]\left(\lambda-H\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{2}\left\|{\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\left[{{\boldsymbol{c}}_{\mathrm{i}},H}\right]\left(\lambda-H\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{2}\\
+\tr\!\left(\mathds{1}_{0<H<\lambda}\left(A^{*}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\,m+m\,A^{*}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A-m\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left|{A}\right|^{2}\right)\right)+\tr\!\left(\left[{A^{*},m}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\right)

where
({\boldsymbol{b}}_{\mathrm{i}},{\boldsymbol{c}}_{\mathrm{i}})_{\mathrm{i}=1\dots 3}=\left((A^{*},A\,m),(m\,A^{*},A),(-m,\left|{A}\right|^{2})\right)
.

Proof of Proposition
5.3
.

Using the cyclicity of the trace

\displaystyle\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{A,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|^{2}m\right)

\displaystyle=\tr\!\left(\left(A^{*}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left|{A}\right|^{2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-A^{*}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-A\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)m\right)

(91)

\displaystyle=\tr\!\left(\left(\mathbf{1}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)\left(A^{*}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\,m+m\,A^{*}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A-m\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left|{A}\right|^{2}\right)+\left[{A^{*},m}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\right).

With the notation
B=A^{*}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\,m+m\,A^{*}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A-m\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left|{A}\right|^{2}
, one can then decompose
\mathbf{1}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\mathds{1}_{0<H<\lambda}+\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}
to get

(92)

\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{A,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|^{2}m\right)=\tr\!\left(\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\,B\right)+\tr\!\left(\mathds{1}_{0<H<\lambda}\,B\right)+\tr\!\left(\left[{A^{*},m}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\right)

and it remains to treat the first term of the right-hand side,
\tr\!\left(\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\,B\right)
, which can be written as a sum of terms of the form
\tr\!\left(\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\,{\boldsymbol{b}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,{\boldsymbol{c}}\right)
. Keeping the same notation as in the proof of Proposition
3.4
we have

\tr\!\left(\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\,{\boldsymbol{b}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,{\boldsymbol{c}}\right)=\sum_{\lambda_{k}\geq\lambda}\sum_{\lambda_{j}\leq 0}\frac{1}{\left(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{j}\right)^{2}}\tr\!\left(\mathbf{P}_{k}\left[{H,{\boldsymbol{b}}}\right]\mathbf{P}_{j}\left[{{\boldsymbol{c}},H}\right]\mathbf{P}_{k}\right).

Following the proof of Proposition
3.4
we get the estimate

\left|{\tr\!\left(\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\,{\boldsymbol{b}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,{\boldsymbol{c}}\right)}\right|\leq\left\|{\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\left[{H,{\boldsymbol{b}}}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\lambda-H\right)^{-1}}\right\|_{2}\left\|{\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\left[{H,{\boldsymbol{c}}}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\lambda-H\right)^{-1}}\right\|_{2}.

The last inequality combined with (
91
) and (
92
) finishes the proof.
∎

Our first step towards the proof of the
L^{2}
regularity is the following lemma, which is based on an application of the weighted Hilbert–Schmidt commutator bound in Proposition
5.3
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\mathds{1}_{H\leq 0}
and
H=-\hbar^{2}\Delta+V
with
d\geq 3
. Then

\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq\frac{\mathcal{C}_{3}}{\hbar}\left(\mathsf{A}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\mathsf{B}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)

with
\mathcal{C}_{3}=3\,\mathcal{C}_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{2}
, and

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\hbar}\left\|{\nabla V\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}\boldsymbol{p},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\frac{1}{\hbar^{2}}\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\lVert\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{5}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}.

Additionally,

\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{\left\|{\left\langle x\right\rangle^{-2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}}{\sqrt{\hbar}}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}\right).

Proof.

Taking
A=\nabla
,
m=\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}
and
\lambda=\hbar
in Proposition
5.3
gives

\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{2}=h^{d}\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{\nabla,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|^{2}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}\right)\leq\mathsf{A}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\tr\!\left(\left(\hbar-H\right)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)+3\,\mathsf{B}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\hbar^{-2}\,\tr\!\left(\mathds{1}_{0<H<\hbar}\right)

where
\mathsf{A}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
and
\mathsf{B}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
are defined in the statement. Therefore, by Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
3.2

\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq\left(3\,\mathcal{C}_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{2}\right)\frac{\mathsf{A}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\mathsf{B}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}{\hbar}\,.

To get the second equation we use the fact that
h^{d}\,\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(x,x)=\nabla\rho
and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality for the trace to get for any
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

with
\left\|{\varphi}\right\|_{L^{2}}=1

\left|{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla\rho\,\varphi}\right|=h^{d}\tr\!\left(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\varphi\right)\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\!x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}\left\|{\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{-2}\varphi}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}.

Finally, we compute
\left\|{\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{-2}\varphi}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}\leq\left\|{\left\langle x\right\rangle^{-2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
and use the triangle inequality.
∎

The previous lemma is sufficient to analyze the region
\left|{x}\right|\leq\hbar
. To complete the proof of the
L^{2}
regularity of
\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
, it remains to look at the cases where
\left|{x}\right|\geq\hbar
. Recall our notation
\mathsf{T}_{x}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\tau_{x}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\tau_{-x}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\mathds{1}_{H\leq 0}
and
H=-\hbar^{2}\Delta+V
with
d\geq 3
. Let
\beta\geq 0
. Then, for any
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\left|{x_{0}}\right|\in(\hbar,1)
,

h^{d}\tr\!\left(\left|{\mathsf{T}_{x_{0}}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right|^{2}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}\right)\leq\mathcal{C}_{4}\left(\mathsf{A}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}+\mathsf{B}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\right)\left|{x_{0}}\right|

where
\mathcal{C}_{4}=2\,\mathcal{C}_{1}+e^{2\beta}\;\mathcal{C}_{2}
, and

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{\hbar}\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|{e^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}\,\nabla V}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\right)\left\|{e^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}\,\nabla V}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=2\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}.

Proof.

Let
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be such that
\left|{x_{0}}\right|\in[\hbar,1]
. Then, taking
A=\tau_{x_{0}}=e^{i\,x_{0}\cdot\boldsymbol{p}/\hbar}
,
m=\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}
and
\lambda=\left|{x_{0}}\right|\leq 1
in Proposition
3.3
, and then using the lemmas
3.2
and
3.1
for
\lambda=\left|{x_{0}}\right|\geq\hbar

h^{d}\tr\!\left(\left|{\mathsf{T}_{x_{0}}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right|^{2}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}\right)\\
\leq 2\,\mathcal{C}_{1}\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{2}}{\left|{x_{0}}\right|^{2}}\left\|{\left(\mathsf{T}_{x_{0}}V-V\right){\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\left[{\tau_{x_{0}}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}.

We now estimate the term
\left|{x_{0}}\right|^{-2}
by extracting a factor
\left|{x_{0}}\right|
from each norm. Notice that by the Leibniz rule for commutators, the fact that
\tau_{x_{0}}
commutes with
\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}
and the unitarity of
\tau_{x_{0}}
, it follows that

\left\|{\left[{\tau_{x_{0}}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\leq\frac{\left|{x_{0}}\right|}{\hbar}\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|{\left(\mathsf{T}_{x_{0}}V-V\right)\tau_{x_{0}}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

where we used
1\leq\frac{\left|{x_{0}}\right|}{\hbar}
.
On the other hand, it follows from a first order Taylor formula that

\left|{\mathsf{T}_{x_{0}}V-V}\right|\leq\left\|{e^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}\nabla V}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left|{x_{0}}\right|\int_{0}^{1}e^{\beta\left|{\left(1-\theta\right)x+\theta x_{0}}\right|}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta\leq\left\|{e^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}\nabla V}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left|{x_{0}}\right|e^{\beta\left|{x_{0}}\right|}.

From this we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|{\left(\mathsf{T}_{x_{0}}V-V\right)\tau_{x_{0}}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\tau_{x_{0}}\left(V-\mathsf{T}_{-x_{0}}V\right)\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|{x_{0}}\right|e^{\beta\left|{x_{0}}\right|}\left\|{e^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}\nabla V}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}},

and similarly

\left\|{\left(\mathsf{T}_{x_{0}}V-V\right){\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\leq\left|{x_{0}}\right|e^{\beta\left|{x_{0}}\right|}\left\|{e^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}\nabla V}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}.

This finishes the proof after we gather all estimates.
∎

The multiple terms appearing in the previous Lemmas are estimated as follows.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\mathds{1}_{H\leq 0}
with
H=-\hbar^{2}+V(x)
in
d\geq 1
. Assume
V
satisfies (
H4
). Then, for any
\beta>0
there exists
C>0
such that

(93)

\displaystyle\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\hbar\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

(94)

\displaystyle\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\hbar\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{3}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

(95)

\displaystyle\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}\boldsymbol{p},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\hbar\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

Additionally,

\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\leq C\left\|{e^{2\beta\left|{x}\right|}\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}.

Proof.

For notational simplicity we write
\left\|{\cdot}\right\|:=\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}
. Additionally, for an operator
A
we let
\nabla A:=\left[{\nabla,A}\right]
and
\Delta A:=\left[{\nabla,\cdot\left[{\nabla,A}\right]}\right]
. First, we compute

(96)

\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},A}\right]{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|\leq\hbar^{2}\left\|{\Delta A\,{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|+2\,\hbar\left\|{\left(\nabla A\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\right){\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|,

and (
93
) follows with
A=V
and
{\boldsymbol{\rho}}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
, and using Hypothesis (
H4
). Next, a similar computation shows

(97)

\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},A}\right]{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|\leq\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},\left[{\boldsymbol{p}^{2},A}\right]}\right]{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|+2\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},A}\right]\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|.

For the first term in (
97
), we use Inequality (
96
) with
\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},A}\right]
instead of
A
so that

\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},\left[{\boldsymbol{p}^{2},A}\right]}\right]{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|\leq\hbar^{2}\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},\Delta A}\right]{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|+2\,\hbar\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},\nabla A\cdot\boldsymbol{p}}\right]{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|

where we used
\Delta\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},B}\right]=\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},\Delta B}\right]
and
\nabla\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},B}\right]=\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},\nabla B}\right]
for any operator
B
. Using again (
96
) we find for the two terms

\displaystyle\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},\Delta A}\right]{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\hbar^{2}\left\|{\Delta^{2}A\,{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|+2\,\hbar\left\|{\nabla\Delta A\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|

\displaystyle\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},\nabla A\cdot\boldsymbol{p}}\right]{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\hbar^{2}\left\|{\left(\nabla\Delta A\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\right){\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|+2\hbar\left\|{\left(\nabla\left(\nabla A\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\right){\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|.

For the second term in (
97
), we use Inequality (
96
) with
\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
instead of
{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
. Putting all together we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},A}{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\hbar^{2}\left(\hbar^{2}\left\|{\Delta^{2}A\,{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|+2\,\hbar\left\|{\left(\nabla\Delta A\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\right){\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|\right)

(98)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\,\hbar\left(\hbar^{2}\left\|{\left(\nabla\Delta A\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\right){\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|+2\,\hbar\left\|{\left(\nabla\left(\nabla A\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\right){\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\left(\hbar^{2}\left\|{\Delta A\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|+2\,\hbar\left\|{\left(\nabla A\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\right)\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|\right).

The bound (
94
) follows by taking
A=V
,
{\boldsymbol{\rho}}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
and using (
H4
). As for the other inequality,

\left\|{\left[{\boldsymbol{p}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},A}\right]{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|\leq\hbar\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},\nabla A}\right]{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|+2\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},A}\right]\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|.

so that (
95
) follows by an appropriate application of (
98
). Finally, for the last bound, we write

\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}\left(H-V\right){\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}H-V\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-2\,\hbar\,\nabla V\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-\Delta V{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,,

from which we conclude

\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|\leq\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|\left\|{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}H}\right\|+\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}V\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|+2\,\hbar\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\,\nabla V\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|+\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\,\Delta V{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|

and the desired bound then follows by using (
H4
).
∎

Finally, we turn to the proof of the main result of this subsection.

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

We consider
d=3
, and separate the proof into three cases. First, when
\left|{x}\right|\leq\hbar
, we use Lemma
5.4
. In order to control the constants
\mathsf{A}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
and
\mathsf{B}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
it suffices to show that

\lVert\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{5}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\,,\quad\frac{1}{\hbar}\left\|{\left[{\boldsymbol{p}\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}},\quad\frac{1}{\hbar}\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{2},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}},\quad\frac{1}{\hbar}\left\|{\left[{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4},V}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

are uniformly bounded in
\hbar
. The first quantity is controlled thanks to
Lemma
2.9
. The other three are controlled thanks to Lemma
5.6
in terms of
\left\|{e^{2\beta\left|{x}\right|}\left\langle\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle^{2}{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}
. The latter is bounded thanks to Agmon’s estimates in Lemma
2.8
. Secondly, for
\hbar\leq\left|{x}\right|\leq 1
, we use Lemma
5.5
. In this case, the control of the constants
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
and
\mathsf{B}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is dealt with analogously. Finally, when
\left|{x}\right|\geq 1
, then we just notice that

\left\|{T_{x}\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq 2\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq 2\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/2}\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}^{1/2}

and we know by Inequality (
25
) and Lemma
2.1
that these quantities are bounded independently of
\hbar
. The bound in
B^{1/2}_{2,\infty}
then follows by definition of Besov spaces and the
H^{s}
bound by the continuous embedding
B^{1/2}_{2,\infty}\subset H^{s}
for
s<1/2
.
∎

6.
Interacting case: Hartree theory

In this section, we consider the case of interacting particles, that is, as described in introduction, the potential is given by

V_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}:=K*\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}+U

with
K(x)=\kappa\left|{x}\right|^{-a}
with
\kappa>0
and
a\in[0,1]
and
U:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
some external potential and which we assume satisfies Hypothesis (
H3
). We study the minimizers
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}:={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\textnormal{H}}
of the Hartree energy functional
\mathscr{E}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
given in Equation (
14
). They satisfy the equation (see e.g.
[
48
]
)

(99)

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\mathds{1}_{H_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}<0}+{\boldsymbol{q}}\quad\text{ with }\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}:=-\hbar^{2}\Delta+V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}

where
0\leq{\boldsymbol{q}}\leq 1
is a self-adjoint operator such that
\ran({\boldsymbol{q}})\subset\ker(H_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}})
. In particular,

(100)

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\leq\mathds{1}_{H_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\leq 0}}\,.

One of the main ingredients in the proof of the commutator estimates will be to prove that the potential
V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
satisfies the assumptions explained of the linear part, i.e. some estimates on its regularity and growth uniform in
\hbar
. This is the goal of the next subsection.

6.1.
A priori estimates

Let us start with recalling some well-known estimates on the convolution
K*\rho
by the singular kernel
K
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
d=3
, and denote
K(x)=\kappa\left|{x}\right|^{-a}
for
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
and
a\in(0,1]
. Let
\rho\in L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}
. Then for all
\delta>0
and
\alpha\in\left(0,1\right)

\displaystyle\left\|{K*\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|{\kappa}\right|\left(\delta^{-a}\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{1}}+C_{a}\,\delta^{d-a}\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla K*\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta}\left|{\kappa}\right|\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\theta}\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{1}}^{1-\theta}

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla K*\rho}\right\|_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|{\kappa}\right|\left(\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{1}}+\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)

with
C_{a}=\tfrac{4\,\pi}{3-a}
,
\theta=\frac{a+1}{3}
and
C_{\theta}=\frac{a}{1-\theta}\,(\frac{4\,\pi}{3\,\theta})^{\theta}
. Additionally, in the case when
a<1
there is a constant
C=C(a)>0
such that

\left\|{\nabla^{2}K*\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left|{\kappa}\right|\left(\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{1}}+\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\right).

Moreover, in the case when
a=1
, there is
C>0
such that

\left\|{\nabla^{2}K*\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{1}}+\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\ln(1+\left\|{\nabla\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}})\right).

Proof.

Observe that for all
a\in(0,1]
we have
\left|{x}\right|^{-a}\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and we split

K*\rho(x)=\int_{\left|{x-y}\right|\leq\delta}\frac{\rho(y)}{\left|{x-y}\right|^{a}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y+\int_{\left|{x-y}\right|\geq\delta}\frac{\rho(y)}{\left|{x-y}\right|^{a}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y\,.

The first integral can be estimated using
\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}
and the
\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y
integral is of order
\delta^{d-a}
. For the second integral we bound
\left|{x-y}\right|^{-a}\leq\delta^{-a}
and use the
\left\|{\rho}\right\|_{L^{1}}
norm. The estimates for the derivatives are identical.

In the case of the Coulomb potential, it is well-known that
\nabla K*\rho
is log-Lipschitz (see e.g.
[
45
,
33
]
) and so in particular Hölder continuous. The last inequality is a variant of the inequality used in
[
5
]
(a proof can be found in
[
35
]
) and can be proved again by splitting the integrals and using
\left\|{\nabla\rho}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}
when
\left|{x-y}\right|
is small.
∎

As a consequence, we obtain the
L^{p}
estimates which depend only on the external trap.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
d=3
and let
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
be as in (
99
). Then, for all
p\in[1,\infty]
there exists
C=C_{\beta,R,a,p}>0
independent of
\hbar
such that

\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{p}}\leq C\left(1+\left\|{U_{-}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}U_{-}^{3/2}\right).

Proof.

Since
K\geq 0
, it follows that
V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\geq U
, and so
\left(V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)_{-}\leq U_{-}
. In particular, Inequality (
100
), Agmon’s estimates and Lemma
2.1
give the exponential decay

\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\leq C_{d,\beta}\left(1+\left\|{U_{-}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}U_{-}^{3/2}\right)

where we also used the
L^{1}
estimate (
25
). Observe now that the right-hand side is independent of
\hbar
and
\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
. As a consequence, we can now bootstrap the
L^{\infty}
estimates for
\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
. More precisely, from Lemma
2.1
and Lemma
6.1

\displaystyle\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{U_{-}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|{U\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|{K*\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{U_{-}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|{Ue^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\delta^{3-a}\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\delta^{-a}\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\right).

Therefore we choose
\delta=\delta(a)>0
small enough and use again Inequality (
25
) to conclude

\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{d,\beta,R,a}\left(1+\left\|{U_{-}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}U_{-}^{3/2}\right).

The estimates for the
L^{1}
norm come from (
25
) with
\kappa\geq 0
. It then suffices to interpolate.
∎

6.1.1.
Commutator estimates

Thanks to the uniform
C^{1,\alpha}
estimates of Lemma
6.1
and
[
47
, Theorem 1.5]
we can find
\varepsilon_{0}>0
small enough so that

(101)

\left|{h^{d}\tr\!\left(\mathds{1}_{H_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\leq E}\right)-\iint_{\left|{\xi}\right|^{2}+V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(x)\leq E}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi}\right|\leq\mathcal{C}_{0}\,\hbar

for all
E\in[-\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0}]
, and all
\hbar\in(0,1)
. In particular, the local eigenvalue estimate contained in Lemma
3.1
implies

(102)

h^{d}\tr\mathds{1}_{[a,b]}(H_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})\leq\mathcal{C}_{1}\left(\left|{b-a}\right|+\hbar\right),\qquad-\varepsilon_{0}\leq a\leq b\leq\varepsilon_{0}\,.

Thanks to
K\geq 0
, the constant
\mathcal{C}_{1}>0
depends only on
\mathcal{C}_{0}>0
, the external trap
U(x)
, and the parameter
\varepsilon_{0}
. We are now ready for the proof of Theorem
1.5
.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

Arguing as in the proof of Theorem
1.1
, it suffices to look at the case
\hbar\leq h_{\beta}:=\min(1,1/8\beta)
. For the proof, let us take
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
as in (
99
) and write

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{q}}\,,\quad\text{ with }\quad\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\mathds{1}_{H_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}<0}\,.

First, let us remove the error term
{\boldsymbol{q}}
. Consider
A=A^{*}
a self-adjoint operator. Then, since
0\leq{\boldsymbol{q}}\leq\mathbf{1}
and
{\boldsymbol{q}}=\mathds{1}_{H_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}=0}\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\leq\mathds{1}_{H_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\leq 0}
and Inequality (
102
), we get

\left\|{\left[{A,{\boldsymbol{q}}}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}\leq 2\left\|{{\boldsymbol{q}}\,A}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}\leq h^{d}\tr{\mathds{1}_{H_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}=0}}\left\|{{\boldsymbol{q}}\,A}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{1}\left\|{A\,\mathds{1}_{H_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\leq 0}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\hbar\,.

Arguing as in the proof of Theorem
1.1
,
\left\|{x\,\mathds{1}_{H_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\leq 0}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}
and
\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}\,\mathds{1}_{H_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\leq 0}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}
are bounded uniformly in
\hbar
. Thus, there exists a constant
C
independent of
\hbar
such that

h^{d}\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{x,{\boldsymbol{q}}}\right]}\right|\right)\leq C\,\hbar\quad\text{ and }\quad h^{d}\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{\boldsymbol{p},{\boldsymbol{q}}}\right]}\right|\right)\leq C\,\hbar\,.

Thanks to Hölder’s inequality for Schatten norms,
\left\|{\left[{A,{\boldsymbol{q}}}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{p}}\leq 2^{1/p^{\prime}}\left\|{A\,{\boldsymbol{q}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{1/p^{\prime}}\left\|{\left[{A,{\boldsymbol{q}}}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}^{1/p}
and we can automatically control the
p>1
norms. Thus, it suffices to analyze
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
and, for notational simplicity, we drop the tilde
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}:=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
throughout the proof. Let us also note that the same argument implies that all estimates contained in Section
3
hold for either
\mathds{1}_{H\leq 0}
and
\mathds{1}_{H<0}
.

Secondly, we look at the Hilbert–Schmidt estimates. A straightforward application of Proposition
3.6
implies that there is
C>0
such that for all

\hbar\in(0,h_{\beta}]

\displaystyle h^{d}\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{x,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\,h\left(\left\|{x\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle h^{d}\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{\boldsymbol{p},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\,h\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right).

Similarly as in the proof of Theorem
1.1
, all terms on the right-hand side can be bounded using either energy estimates, or Agmon’s estimates. Note that here we have thanks to Lemma
6.1

(103)

\left\|{\nabla V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\leq C_{\beta}\left\|{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+C_{\beta}\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

which is controlled with Agmon’s estimates, for all
0\leq a\leq 1
.

Thirdly, for the trace-class estimates we apply Proposition
3.7
for
\mu=\left\|{U_{-}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+1
to find that there is
C>0
such that for all
\hbar\in(0,h_{\beta}]

(104)

\displaystyle h^{d}\tr{\left|{\left[{x,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\hbar\left(\left\|{x\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|{\nabla V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle h^{d}\tr{\left|{\left[{\boldsymbol{p},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\hbar\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|{\nabla^{2}V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(x){\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|{\nabla^{2}V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\right).

Let
0\leq a<1
. Then, the estimate for the first derivative (
103
) also holds for two derivatives. We repeat the same argument as before and conclude that most terms are uniformly bounded in
\hbar
. The only exception is the following mixed term which, thanks to hypotheses (
H2
) and (
H3
), and Lemma
6.1
, satisfies the bound

\left\|{\nabla^{2}V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\leq C_{\kappa,a}\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+C_{\beta}\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}.

It suffices to estimate
\lVert e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\,\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}
, to
which we use the generalized Agmon’s estimates from Lemma
2.5
.

Consider now
a=1
. First, we observe that the argument for the position estimates in (
104
) is unchanged, since it only requires one bounded derivative. On the other hand, for the momentum we use the second trace-class bounds in Proposition
3.5
and Lemma
3.8
to compute for
\mu=1+\left\|{U_{-}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}
and any
\lambda\geq\hbar

h^{d}\tr{\left|{\left[{\boldsymbol{p},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|}\leq C\left(\lambda\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\frac{\hbar}{\lambda}\left(\left\|{\nabla^{2}V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|{\nabla^{2}V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(x){\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\right)\right).

We split
V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=U+K*\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
and obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla^{2}V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(x){\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{e^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}\nabla^{2}U}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|{\nabla^{2}K*\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla^{2}V_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{p}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{e^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}\nabla^{2}U}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\boldsymbol{p}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|{\nabla^{2}K*\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{p}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}.

By assumption and thanks to Agmon’s estimates, the
U
-dependent terms are uniformly bounded in
\hbar
. On the other hand, thanks to Lemma
6.1
and the
L^{p}
estimates for the density
\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
we get

\left\|{\nabla^{2}K*\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\ln(1+\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}})\,.

Thanks to Lemma
5.1
we also have the
W^{1,\infty}
bound

\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{C_{d}}{\hbar}\,\Big(1+\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}^{5/4}\Big)\,.

Moreover, arguing similarly as above, Lemma
2.7
and Lemma
6.1
yields that
\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}
is bounded uniformly in
\hbar
. Putting the last five bounds together, we get, for an appropriate constant
C>0
independent of
\hbar

h^{d}\tr{\left|{\left[{\boldsymbol{p},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|}\leq C\,\Big(\lambda+\frac{\hbar}{\lambda}\left|{\ln\hbar}\right|\Big)\,.

This finishes the proof by taking
\lambda=\hbar\left|{\ln\hbar}\right|^{1/2}\geq\hbar
.
∎

6.2.
Weyl’s law

Let us now turn to the proof of the local Weyl law in the non-linear setting. Recall the notation

\mathscr{E}_{f}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}(\left|{\xi}\right|^{2}+U(x))+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho_{f}\left(K*\varrho_{f}\right)

for any function
f\in L^{1}
for which
\mathcal{E}_{f}<\infty
. Recall as well
\varrho_{f}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(x,\xi)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi
,
\widetilde{f}=G_{\varepsilon}*f
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\rho}}=G_{\varepsilon}\star{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
. As in the linear case, we also introduce the following classical and quantum errors

\displaystyle Q(g)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\left|{\mathcal{H}_{g}}\right|\left|{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{g}\leq 0}-g}\right|

\displaystyle Q_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\boldsymbol{\rho}})

\displaystyle:=h^{d}\tr\!\left(\left|{H_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right|\left|{{\boldsymbol{\rho}}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right|^{2}+\left|{H_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right|({\boldsymbol{\rho}}-{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{2})\right)

for any classical function
0\leq g\leq 1
, and any operator
0\leq{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\leq\mathbf{1}
. First, we establish the following general bound in analogy to the linear case.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
0\leq f\leq 1
and
0\leq{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\leq\mathbf{1}
be minimizers of
\mathscr{E}_{f}
and
\mathscr{E}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
, respectively, in
d\geq 3
. Let
p=1+\frac{2}{d}
,
c_{d}=1/\omega^{2/d}
and for any function
0\leq g\leq 1
denote

\mathcal{H}_{g}(x,\xi)=\left|{\xi}\right|^{2}-c_{d}\,\varrho_{g}^{p-1}\,.

Then, we have that

Q_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\,\tilde{\!{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}_{f})+Q(\tilde{f}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})+\frac{c_{d}}{p^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|{\varrho_{\,\tilde{\!{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}}^{p/2}-\varrho_{f}^{p/2}}\right|^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}U_{+}\varrho_{\,\tilde{\!{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}}\leq\mathscr{E}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}-\mathscr{E}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\mathscr{E}_{\tilde{f}}-\mathscr{E}_{f}\,.

Proof.

We split the proof in two parts. Let us recall first that for any
{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
the quantitative variational principle in Lemma
4.3
gives

(105)

Q_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\boldsymbol{\rho}})\leq\mathscr{E}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}-\mathscr{E}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,.

Secondly, using
\varrho_{f}=\omega_{d}\left(U+K*\varrho_{f}\right)_{-}^{d/2}
and considering now an arbitrary function
0\leq g\leq 1
, one obtains

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{g}-\mathscr{E}_{f}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\varrho_{g}-\varrho_{f}\right)K*\left(\varrho_{g}-\varrho_{f}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\left|{\xi}\right|^{2}\left(g-f\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(U+K*\varrho_{f}\right)\left(\varrho_{g}-\varrho_{f}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\left|{\xi}\right|^{2}\left(g-f\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(U+K*\varrho_{f}\right)_{+}\varrho_{g}-c_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho_{f}^{\frac{2}{d}}\left(\varrho_{g}-\varrho_{f}\right).

Therefore, using the fact that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}f\left|{\xi}\right|^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi=\frac{c_{d}}{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho_{f}^{p}
and
\widehat{K}\geq 0
, it gives

(106)

\mathscr{E}_{g}-\mathscr{E}_{f}\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}U_{+}\varrho_{g}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\left(\left|{\xi}\right|^{2}-c_{d}\,\varrho_{g}^{p-1}\right)g+\frac{c_{d}}{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}p\,\varrho_{g}^{p}-\varrho_{f}^{p}-p\,\varrho_{f}^{p-1}\left(\varrho_{g}-\varrho_{f}\right)

where we also used
\left(U+K*\varrho_{f}\right)_{+}\varrho_{g}\geq U_{+}\varrho_{g}
. Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
4.6
we use the "quantitative bathtub principle" as in Lemma
4.3
to get

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\mathcal{H}_{g}\,g=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\left|{\mathcal{H}_{g}}\right|\left|{g-\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{g}\leq 0}}\right|-\frac{c_{d}}{p^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho_{g}^{p}\,.

Combined with (
106
) and using
1-1/p^{\prime}=1/p
we find

(107)

\mathscr{E}_{g}-\mathscr{E}_{f}\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}U_{+}\varrho_{g}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\left|{\mathcal{H}_{g}}\right|\left|{g-\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{g}\leq 0}}\right|+\frac{c_{d}}{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho_{g}^{p}-\varrho_{f}^{p}-p\,\varrho_{f}^{p-1}\left(\varrho_{g}-\varrho_{f}\right).

We finish the proof by taking
{\boldsymbol{\rho}}={\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{\tilde{f}}
,
g=f_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}
, putting together (
105
) and (
107
), and employing Young’s inequality.
∎

In order to connect our analysis back to the linear case we make the following observation. Since
\varrho_{\tilde{f}}=g_{\varepsilon}*\varrho_{f}
,
\widehat{K}\geq 0
and
0\leq\widehat{g}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\leq 1
, it follows from Plancherel formula that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho_{\tilde{f}}\,K*\varrho_{\tilde{f}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\widehat{K}\,\widehat{g}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left|{\widehat{\rho}_{f}}\right|^{2}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho_{f}\,K*\varrho_{f}

and similarly for
\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
. Therefore, arguing as in the linear case, one finds

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\tilde{f}}-\mathscr{E}_{f}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d\,h^{2}}{8\pi\,\varepsilon}M_{f}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(g_{\varepsilon}*U-U\right)\varrho_{f}

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\,\tilde{\!{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}}-\mathscr{E}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d\,h^{2}}{8\pi\,\varepsilon}M_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(g_{\varepsilon}*U-U\right)\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,,

with
M_{f}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}f
and
M_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=h^{d}\tr\!\left({\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)
. The expression on the right-hand side was analyzed in detail in Lemma
4.6
for
V=U\in W^{2,q}(\Omega_{1})
where
\Omega_{1}=\{x:\dist\!\left(x,\{U\leq 0\}\right)\leq 1\}
, which led to Proposition
4.7
. We record here the analogous result.

Lemma 6.4
.

Under the same conditions of Proposition
6.3

Q_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\,\tilde{\!{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}_{f})+Q(\tilde{f}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})+\frac{c_{d}}{p^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|{\varrho_{\,\tilde{\!{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}}^{p/2}-\varrho_{f}^{p/2}}\right|^{2}\leq\mathsf{M}\,\frac{h^{2}}{\varepsilon}+\mathsf{D}^{\prime}\varepsilon\,.

Here, we employ the following notations for any
\beta\geq 0

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{8\pi}\left(M_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+M_{f}\right)

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{d}{\pi}\left\|{U}\right\|_{W^{2,1}(\Omega_{1})}\left(\left\|{\varrho_{f}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)+C_{d,\beta}\left\|{e^{-\beta\left|{x}\right|}\nabla U_{+}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|{e^{\beta\left|{x}\right|}\nabla\varrho_{f}}\right\|_{L^{1}}

where
C_{d,\beta}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|{x}\right|^{2}e^{-\pi\left|{x}\right|^{2}+\beta\left|{x}\right|}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x
.

With these bounds, we can replicate the proof in the linear case and obtain the proof of Theorem
1.6
.

Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

For the proof, denote for simplicity
f:=f_{\textnormal{TF}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}:={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\textnormal{H}}
the minimizers of the classical and quantum energies, respectively. We let
K
and
U
satisfy hypotheses (
H2
) and (
H3
), respectively, with parameter
\beta>0
. Without loss of generality, we may assume
\hbar\leq 1/8\beta
. Otherwise we can proceed as in the proof of Theorem
1.1
and modify the value of the overall constant.

First, we prove the estimates for the densities. Starting from Lemma
6.4
we follow the proof of Proposition
4.1
. We find that there exists
C>0
such that

(108)

\displaystyle\left\|{\varrho_{f}-\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,h^{1/3}\left(1+\left\|{\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{1/2}_{2,\infty}}\right)^{2/3}\left(1+\mathsf{L}_{2}\,\mathsf{M}^{1/2}+\mathsf{L}_{2}\,\mathsf{D}^{\prime 1/2}h^{1/3}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|{\varrho_{f}-\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,h^{1/2}\left(1+\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}^{1/2}+\mathsf{D}^{\prime 1/2}\right)\left(1+\mathsf{L}_{1}+\mathsf{M}^{1/2}\right)

where
\mathsf{L}_{1}
and
\mathsf{L}_{2}
are defined as in (
75
) and (
76
). All quantities
\mathsf{M}
,
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}
,
\mathsf{L}_{1}
and
\mathsf{L}_{2}
can be bounded uniformly in
\hbar
thanks to either
L^{p}
or Agmon’s estimates. The desired bound then follows by the different estimates on
\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}
that arise in the cases
0\leq a<1
and
a=1
. See e.g. Remark
1.4.9
.

For the convergence of states, start from Lemma
6.4
and follow the proof Proposition
4.2
. We obtain, in terms of the Husimi measure
m_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
, that for some constant
C>0

\displaystyle\left\|{f-m_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq h^{1/2}\left(\left(\mathsf{L}_{1}+\mathsf{C}_{1}\right)\left(\mathsf{M}+\mathsf{D}^{\prime}\right)+\mathsf{C}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|{f-f_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,h^{1/4}\left\|{f}\right\|_{\dot{B}_{2,\infty}^{1/2}}+\left\|{f-m_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\left\|{{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{f}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,h^{1/2}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}+\left\|{\nabla f}\right\|_{\mathcal{M}}\right)+\left\|{f-m_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}

where
\mathsf{C}_{1}
and
\mathsf{C}_{2}
are defined in (
77
) and (
78
) in terms of
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
and
U(x)
.
They are uniformly bounded in
\hbar
thanks to
L^{p}
estimates. Arguing as in the linear case,
we know that

f\in\dot{B}_{2,\infty}^{1/2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})
and
\nabla f\in\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{6})
.
Thus, we now use the estimates for the quantum gradient
\left\|{\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{1}}
that follow from Theorem
1.5
.

Finally, we note that the potential

V_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}=U(x)+K*\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}

verifies Hypothesis (
H4
). To see this, first, thanks to Lemma
2.7
, we obtain that
\lVert\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{4}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}
is bounded uniformly in
\hbar
, thanks to Lemma
6.1
, and Hypothesis (
H4
). Therefore, thanks to Lemma
5.1
we obtain

\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C/\hbar\quad\text{ and }\quad\left\|{\nabla^{2}\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C/\hbar^{2}\,.

Thus, similarly, we can now use Lemma
2.9
to obtain

\left\|{\left|{\boldsymbol{p}}\right|^{6}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}}\leq C\,.

We conclude that all the moments in Lemma
5.1
are uniformly bounded in
\hbar
. Let us set
W_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\left|{x}\right|^{-1}*\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}
. Thanks to well-known elliptic-type estimates satisfied by the Coulomb potential, recorded in Lemma
6.1
, we obtain

\left\|{W_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\,,\quad\left\|{\nabla W_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\,,\quad\left\|{\nabla^{2}W_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left(1+\left|{\ln\hbar}\right|\right),

in view of
\ln\left\|{\nabla\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left(1+\left|{\ln\hbar}\right|\right)
. Similarly, for higher derivatives

\nabla^{n}W_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\nabla^{2}(\left|{x}\right|^{-1}*\nabla^{n-2}\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}})\,.

and so for
n=5

\left|{\nabla^{5}W_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right|\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\nabla^{3}\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}+\left\|{\nabla^{3}\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(1+\ln(1+\left\|{\nabla^{3}\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}})\right)\right)\leq C\,\frac{1+\left|{\ln\hbar}\right|}{\hbar^{3}}\,.

By assumption, the external trap
U(x)
verifies the same estimate for
n=2
and
n=5
. We conclude the same is true for
V_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}
. Finally, it now suffices to interpolate between
2\leq n\leq 5
. Therefore, we use Proposition
5.2
to conclude that
\left\|{\varrho_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}}\right\|_{\dot{B}_{2,\infty}^{1/2}}
is uniformly bounded in
\hbar
. We plug this estimate back in (
108
). This gives the desired estimate and finishes the proof.
∎

Appendix A
Another proof of the Hilbert–Schmidt commutator estimate

We give here another proof of a Hilbert–Schmidt commutator estimate which does not use the decomposition of
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
under eigenspaces.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}:=\mathds{1}_{H\leq 0}
and
A
be an operator such that
A\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
and
A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
are trace class. Then for any
\lambda>0
,

\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{A,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{B+\sqrt{B^{2}+4\,D^{2}}}{2}\leq B+D,

with

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\tr\!\left(\left|{\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\left[{H,A^{*}}\right]}\right|^{2}\left(H-\lambda\right)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\tr\!\left(\left|{\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\left[{H,A}\right]}\right|^{2}\left(H-\lambda\right)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=\tr\!\left(\left(\left|{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A}\right|^{2}+\left|{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A^{*}}\right|^{2}\right)\mathds{1}_{0<H<\lambda}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In the particular case when
A
is a normal operator, then the same inequality holds with

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=2\,\tr\!\left(\left|{\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\left[{H,A}\right]}\right|^{2}\left(H-\lambda\right)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=2\,\tr\!\left(\left|{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A}\right|^{2}\mathds{1}_{0<H<\lambda}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

Using the cyclicity of the trace and the fact that
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
gives

\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{A,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|^{2}\right)=\tr\!\left(\left(\mathbf{1}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\right)+\tr\!\left(\left(\mathbf{1}-{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)A\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A^{*}\right)=:I_{1}+I_{2}\,.

Let us look at
I_{1}
. It holds

I_{1}=\tr\!\left(A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\,\mathds{1}_{0<H<\lambda}\right)+\tr\!\left(A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\,\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\right).

Since
\lambda>0
,
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(H-\lambda\right)^{-1}
is well-defined by functional calculus and so

\tr\!\left(A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\,\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\right)=\tr\!\left(A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(H-\lambda\right)^{-1}\left(H-\lambda^{\prime}\right)A\,\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\right)\\
=\tr\!\left(A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(H-\lambda\right)^{-1}\left[{H,A}\right]\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\right)+\tr\!\left(A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(H-\lambda\right)^{-1}A\,\left(H-\lambda\right)\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\right).

Since
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(H-\lambda\right)^{-1}\leq 0
and
\left(H-\lambda\right)\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\geq 0
, the last term of the above equation is nonpositive, which leads to

\tr\!\left(A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\,\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\right)\leq\tr\!\left(A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(H-\lambda\right)^{-1}\left[{H,A}\right]\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\right).

Moreover, since
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}
and
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}=0
, one observes that
\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\,A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\left[{A^{*},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
, and so

\displaystyle\tr\!\left(A^{*}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\,A\,\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tr\!\left(\left[{A^{*},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(H-\lambda\right)^{-1}\left[{H,A}\right]\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},A}\right]}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\tr\!\left(\left|{\mathds{1}_{H\geq\lambda}\left[{H,A^{*}}\right]}\right|^{2}\left(H-\lambda\right)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where the last inequality follows from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the cyclicity of the trace. The same inequality holds for
I_{2}
up to replacing
A
by
A^{*}
, which yields finally

\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{A,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\right]}\right|^{2}\right)\leq\tr\!\left(\left|{\left[{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},A}\right]}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}B+D\,.

Solving the above inequality finishes the proof.
∎
